{"parentUuid":null,"isSidechain":true,"promptId":"c7c0b4cd-e6c6-4488-a68b-ca4f4169315f","agentId":"ab85e5582d27cf290","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"You are an INDEPENDENT adversarial security reviewer — a SECOND pass, separate from the primary red-pen — on SIGIL Phase 9 \"COMPANION\" (the phone companion for a local-first, offense-free personal AI orchestrator). Repo /home/kali/sigil, branch `phase9-companion`. Diff vs main: `cd /home/kali/sigil && git diff main..HEAD` (Phase 9 = commits on top of main 1ffe746). Assume the primary reviewer covers the obvious lenses (forge/replay/rebind on the device-arm and transport). YOUR job: find what they MISS — subtle, second-order, cross-slice integration, and correctness/robustness bugs. Report only; do NOT fix. You MAY write throwaway repro scripts under the scratchpad and run them with `~/.sigil/venv/bin/python` to PROVE a defect.\n\nRead the diff, then read: `sigil/bridge/{envelope.py,server.py,daemon.py,__init__.py}`, `sigil/gesture/{session.py,remote.py,run.py}`, `sigil/bridge/webapp/{app.js,canonical.js,service-worker.js,index.html,manifest.json}`, `sigil/cli.py`, `sigil/mesh/registry.py`, `sigil/agents/approvals.py`, `sigil/reuse/crypto.py`, `sigil/spine/store.py`, `sigil/governor/killswitch.py`, `sigil/perception/recall.py`.\n\nHunt specifically in these under-examined areas (build a runnable repro where you can):\n\n- **The arm CONSUMPTION path (cross-process).** `submit_arm_request` RECORDS a device-signed arm request on the spine; `arm_by_device` is the enforcer. But WHO calls arm_by_device from the recorded request in production? Trace `run_gesture` / the gesture daemon — is there actually a wired path that reads a recorded `gesture.arm_request` and calls `arm_by_device`, or is the remote-arm END-TO-END path INERT (built but never invoked)? If inert, that's an honesty gap (the feature can't actually arm a phone yet) — state it plainly. Also: can a recorded-but-stale arm request (recorded fresh, consumed >30s later) be consumed, and is that fail-safe or a surprise?\n\n- **Kill-switch consult COST + correctness.** `SessionGate.handle` now calls `KillSwitch(store).is_engaged()` on EVERY intent — including per-frame pointer moves at up to 30fps. Is `is_engaged()` an O(n) spine scan? Quantify: at 30fps over a growing append-only spine, is this a self-DoS? (The existing `handle` also calls `classify()` per frame — note whether that's already a subprocess, so whether this is incremental.) Propose whether it should be throttled/cached and what latency that costs the panic guarantee.\n\n- **Nonce/high-water semantics in the envelope.** `device_nonce_highwater` scans `mesh.request` receipt records. Edge cases: two authorized devices sharing a nonce space? a device whose FIRST request uses nonce=0 (default high-water is 0, and `> 0` fails)? nonce as a non-int / float / negative / huge? Does `consume(effectful=True)` record the receipt BEFORE or AFTER the effect — if the effect (relay) throws after the receipt is written, is the nonce burned (a valid command lost) or re-usable (replay)? Prove the ordering.\n\n- **`submit_device_approval` / `submit_arm_request` payload spread.** Both do `payload={**request, ...}`. Can a caller smuggle EXTRA keys into the appended spine record (e.g. a `supersedes_id`, a forged `seq`, a `signal` collision, an oversized blob) that corrupts later queries or the approval/pending logic? Does `submit_device_approval`'s `supersedes_id=payload.get(\"target_seq\")` let a device approval supersede an ARBITRARY seq (e.g. supersede a critical record) — is target_seq constrained to an actual queued item?\n\n- **Endpoint↔action binding + the /api/action & /api/gesture/arm exception.** These two POSTs use the device-signed BODY as the credential (no envelope). Confirm they still can't be driven cross-origin (the anti-rebind gate) AND that a `governor.approval` body can't be replayed to re-approve, and an arm body can't be replayed to spam recorded arm requests (does anything dedupe recorded arm requests, or can an attacker with one captured arm body flood the spine with `gesture.arm_request` records → spine bloat / DoS)?\n\n- **Canonicalization: the SIGNED-CORE extraction.** `arm_request_message` and `envelope_message` rebuild the core from a fixed key tuple — so unsigned extra keys are ignored (good). But verify the SERVER/daemon record the FULL request (including unsigned extras) while VERIFYING only the core — can an attacker attach unsigned extra fields that are then trusted downstream (e.g. an unsigned `ttl_seconds` outside the core, or an unsigned `armed_by`/`device_pubkey` the record trusts)? Cross-check exactly which fields are in `_ARM_CORE` vs which fields the appended `gesture.session_armed` record copies and later trusts.\n\n- **PWA correctness.** In `app.js`/`canonical.js`: the `ts` is floored to int seconds — does the server freshness window (±120s) plus the arm freshness (±30s) tolerate clock skew between phone and PC? Does the offline outbox re-sign or replay stale envelopes? Is the service worker caching `/api/*` (it must not)? Does the approval `msg_digest` / `pubkey` base64 exactly match what `verify_approval` expects (standard vs urlsafe, padding)? Any XSS sink (innerHTML with server data) that could run script under the strict CSP?\n\n- **bind_ok completeness.** IPv6 link-local/ULA? A private IPv4 that's actually routable? `bind_ok` uses `is_private`/`is_loopback` — does it miss anything a real WG setup needs, or ALLOW something it shouldn't (e.g. 169.254/CGNAT/multicast)?\n\nFor each real defect: severity, exact file:line, a concrete repro (script + actual output ideally), the doctrine/contract violated, and a fix direction. Rank by severity. If the strongest attacks fail, say precisely what you tried and why it held. Be concrete and honest — a confirmed low/robustness bug beats a hand-wavy \"maybe critical\". End by running the full suite and reporting totals."},"uuid":"29a99a5d-7aad-496c-8e0a-b93cd84884d2","timestamp":"2026-07-19T00:30:36.253Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"d86d0962-81cb-4d1a-acfe-b8e1d6452922","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"ab85e5582d27cf290","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- crucible: How to operate CRUCIBLE — the authorized, prove-don't-guess offensive-security engine in framework/v2. Read this BEFORE running an engagement/scan, re-verifying a finding, inspecting the event spine, reasoning about the veracity/anti-hallucination layer, the learning/metacognition core, or the safety stack. Use it when a task names CRUCIBLE, OBSIDIAN, `python3 -m framework.v2 <subcommand>`, findings/oracles/evidence certificates, the blackboard event spine, critics, reflection, or the reward/calibration loop.\n- deep-research: Deep research harness — fan-out web searches, fetch sources, adversarially verify claims, synthesize a cited report. - When the user wants a deep, multi-source, fact-checked research report on any topic. BEFORE invoking, check if the question is specific enough to research directly — if underspecified (e.g., \"what car to buy\" without budget/use-case/region), ask 2-3 clarifying questions to narrow scope. Then pass the refined question as args, weaving the answers in.\n- adversarial-sweep: Independent multi-lens adversarial re-attack of a defensive-oracle domain — the FORGE stage-9 companion to RED-PEN. Attack from disjoint lenses, refute each objection with a real repro, report only what survives. - Before merging any AEGIS/FORGE domain stream, alongside (not instead of) the red-pen agent. Pass the domain spec via args. On the email-auth stream it caught 3 CRITICAL/LOW defects PAST a RED-PEN PASS — near-zero-FP cannot be self-certified by one reviewer.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts.\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact page can be granted — behavior static HTML cannot provide on its own, such as the page reading live or connected data, keeping state shared across viewers, or updating and republishing itself. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever the user asks for an artifact needing any such runtime behavior.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- verify: Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by exercising it end-to-end and observing behavior — drive the affected flow, not just tests or typecheck. Run before committing nontrivial changes; bootstraps this repo's project verify skill if none exists yet. Don't invoke it on a diff that only touches tests, docs, or other code with no runtime surface to drive (a change to product source always has one) — there's nothing to observe.\n- code-review: Review the current diff for correctness bugs and reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud (requires claude.ai account access)). Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review.\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a GitHub pull request; for your working diff use /code-review\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch","skillCount":19,"isInitial":true,"names":["crucible","deep-research","adversarial-sweep","dataviz","artifact-design","artifact-capabilities","update-config","keybindings-help","verify","code-review","simplify","fewer-permission-prompts","loop","schedule","claude-api","run","init","review","security-review"]},"type":"attachment","uuid":"794f2006-c780-44d9-a09a-9b7320b266d7","timestamp":"2026-07-19T00:30:36.263Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"b6fdafb5-c8cb-486c-ad74-8a1f866b6662","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"c7c0b4cd-e6c6-4488-a68b-ca4f4169315f","agentId":"ab85e5582d27cf290","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01THxZaBXx5UZhen2fb422Tj","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Device-signed, replay-resistant request envelope (Phase 9 W1-A) — the bridge auth keystone.\n2\t\n3\tThe phone (a device the owner AUTHORIZED via `sigil.mesh.authorize_device`) signs EACH request to the\n4\tdesktop bridge with ITS OWN Ed25519 key over the canonical envelope core. There is NO wire bearer\n5\tsecret: authentication IS the signature, verified against the owner-minted authorized-device set. This\n6\tmirrors the approval-record pattern (`agents.approvals._approval_message` / `verify_approval`) — same\n7\tcanonical-JSON message, same fail-closed `verify_one`, same trust pinned to owner-minted device keys\n8\t(a rogue/revoked device cannot self-authorize; a tampered field breaks the signature).\n9\t\n10\tReplay resistance is a deterministic, spine-anchored monotonic-nonce highwater PER DEVICE: an effectful\n11\trequest must carry a nonce strictly greater than the highest nonce that device has ever been receipted\n12\tfor. Reads still receipt (advancing the watermark) but skip the freshness gate (they have no side\n13\teffect). The receipt is an append-only spine event, so the watermark is tamper-evident memory.\n14\t\n15\tThis module is PURE by construction — NO wallclock, NO RNG. The caller supplies `nonce` and `ts` so the\n16\tJS phone client and Python reproduce the exact signed bytes (the parity contract) and so freshness is a\n17\tdeterministic function of the spine. A wallclock timestamp-WINDOW check (is `ts` recent?) belongs to\n18\tthe server transport layer, not here. Offense-free.\"\"\"\n19\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n20\t\n21\tfrom typing import Set, Tuple, Union\n22\t\n23\tfrom ..reuse import canonical_json, sign, verify_one\n24\tfrom ..spine.store import SpineStore\n25\t\n26\t# The request vocabulary the bridge accepts — a fail-closed allow-list. `panic`/`relay` are effectful;\n27\t# the `read:*` family is side-effect-free. An action outside this set is refused even if validly signed.\n28\tACTIONS = frozenset({\"panic\", \"relay\", \"read:snapshot\", \"read:pending\", \"read:record\",\n29\t                     \"read:stream\", \"read:recall\"})\n30\t\n31\t# The spine signal marking a consumed request. Its records form the per-device nonce highwater.\n32\tRECEIPT_SIGNAL = \"mesh.request\"\n33\t\n34\t\n35\tdef envelope_message(core: dict) -> bytes:\n36\t    \"\"\"The exact bytes signed/verified — canonical JSON of the core. This is the JS<->Python parity\n37\t    contract: a future JS client MUST produce these identical bytes. Mirrors\n38\t    `agents.approvals._approval_message` (return bytes; encode only if `canonical_json` gave a str).\"\"\"\n39\t    m = canonical_json(core)\n40\t    return m if isinstance(m, bytes) else m.encode()\n41\t\n42\t\n43\tdef build_core(device_pubkey: str, action: str, args: dict, nonce, ts) -> dict:\n44\t    \"\"\"The signed core. `nonce`/`ts` are caller-supplied (this module never reads a clock or RNG).\"\"\"\n45\t    return {\"v\": 1, \"device\": device_pubkey, \"action\": action, \"args\": args, \"nonce\": nonce, \"ts\": ts}\n46\t\n47\t\n48\tdef sign_envelope(device_key, core: dict) -> dict:\n49\t    \"\"\"Phone/test side: attach a DEVICE signature over the canonical core, producing the wire payload.\"\"\"\n50\t    return {**core, \"sig\": sign(device_key.private_key_b64, envelope_message(core))}\n51\t\n52\t\n53\tdef verify_envelope(payload, authorized: Set[str]) -> Tuple[bool, Union[dict, str]]:\n54\t    \"\"\"Fail-closed authentication. Rebuild the core (payload minus `sig`); refuse if there is no sig /\n55\t    no device / the device is not in the owner-minted authorized set; then Ed25519-verify the signature\n56\t    over the canonical core; finally require the (now-authenticated) action to be in the `ACTIONS`\n57\t    allow-list. Mirrors `agents.approvals.verify_approval`. Returns (True, core) or (False, reason).\"\"\"\n58\t    p = payload.payload if hasattr(payload, \"payload\") else payload\n59\t    sig = p.get(\"sig\")\n60\t    device = p.get(\"device\")\n61\t    if not sig or not device:\n62\t        return False, \"missing signature or device\"\n63\t    if device not in authorized:                       # trust pinned to owner-minted device keys\n64\t        return False, \"device is not authorized\"\n65\t    core = {k: v for k, v in p.items() if k != \"sig\"}\n66\t    if not verify_one(device, envelope_message(core), sig):\n67\t        return False, \"signature invalid\"\n68\t    if core.get(\"action\") not in ACTIONS:              # allow-list the authenticated action (fail-closed)\n69\t        return False, f\"unknown action: {core.get('action')!r}\"\n70\t    return True, core\n71\t\n72\t\n73\tdef record_receipt(store: SpineStore, core: dict) -> int:\n74\t    \"\"\"Append the append-only consumption receipt. Carries only the routing fields — never `args`\n75\t    (which may hold a subject) and never the signature; the record is auto-tier (an A0 event).\"\"\"\n76\t    return store.append(kind=\"event\", source=\"mesh\", actor=\"DEVICE\",\n77\t                        payload={\"signal\": RECEIPT_SIGNAL, \"device\": core[\"device\"],\n78\t                                 \"action\": core[\"action\"], \"nonce\": core[\"nonce\"],\n79\t                                 \"ts\": core[\"ts\"], \"tier\": \"A0\", \"decision\": \"auto\"})\n80\t\n81\t\n82\tdef device_nonce_highwater(store: SpineStore, device_pubkey: str) -> int:\n83\t    \"\"\"The highest receipted nonce for this device (0 if none). Non-int nonces are tolerated (skipped).\"\"\"\n84\t    hi = 0\n85\t    for r in store.iter_records():\n86\t        p = r.payload\n87\t        if p.get(\"signal\") == RECEIPT_SIGNAL and p.get(\"device\") == device_pubkey:\n88\t            try:\n89\t                n = int(p.get(\"nonce\"))\n90\t            except (TypeError, ValueError):\n91\t                continue\n92\t            if n > hi:\n93\t                hi = n\n94\t    return hi\n95\t\n96\t\n97\tdef consume(store: SpineStore, payload, authorized: Set[str], *, effectful: bool) -> dict:\n98\t    \"\"\"Authenticate, gate replay for effectful requests, receipt, return the core. Raises `ValueError`\n99\t    with the refusal reason on any failure (fail-closed). An effectful request requires a nonce strictly\n100\t    fresher than this device's highwater; a read skips that gate but still receipts (advancing it).\"\"\"\n101\t    ok, core = verify_envelope(payload, authorized)\n102\t    if not ok:\n103\t        raise ValueError(core)\n104\t    if effectful and int(core[\"nonce\"]) <= device_nonce_highwater(store, core[\"device\"]):\n105\t        raise ValueError(\"replay: nonce not fresh\")\n106\t    record_receipt(store, core)\n107\t    return core\n108\t"}]},"uuid":"26851fe0-1786-4243-bf80-578e1ff4dbfb","timestamp":"2026-07-19T00:30:56.658Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b6fdafb5-c8cb-486c-ad74-8a1f866b6662","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"1d586ae7-e2ef-482c-b632-1414b4accfef","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"c7c0b4cd-e6c6-4488-a68b-ca4f4169315f","agentId":"ab85e5582d27cf290","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01McPa6ci11SpzWKiaUuWqrN","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"The SIGIL bridge server (Phase 9 W1-B) — the WireGuard-bound HTTP transport that lets an\n2\tAUTHORIZED phone reach the desktop `BridgeDaemon`. It forks the WS-C glass-cockpit server\n3\t(`sigil.ui.server`) verbatim in shape (ThreadingHTTPServer + BaseHTTPRequestHandler, the strict\n4\tCSP/nosniff/no-referrer header set, the body cap + 30s timeout, the never-log-auth `log_message`,\n5\tthe SSE structure, the anti-DNS-rebinding Host/Origin/Referer gate) and changes ONLY three things:\n6\t\n7\t  • THE BIND. It binds a `bind_ok` address (loopback or a PRIVATE/WireGuard address) — NEVER\n8\t    0.0.0.0 / a public address. The constructor asserts this; the CLI asserts it too. This is the\n9\t    non-negotiable exposure guard: the tunnel, not the transport, is the network boundary.\n10\t\n11\t  • THE AUTH. There is NO wire bearer secret (the ui's printed token is gone). Authentication IS a\n12\t    per-request Ed25519 signature the phone makes with ITS OWN owner-authorized device key — the\n13\t    Wave-1 device envelope (`bridge.envelope`). The server verifies the signature against the\n14\t    owner-minted authorized-device set (recomputed PER REQUEST so a revocation takes effect at once),\n15\t    binds the authenticated envelope `action` to the endpoint it hit (a `read:pending` envelope can\n16\t    never reach the more-sensitive `read:recall`), applies a wallclock timestamp-freshness window\n17\t    (injectable clock), and for EFFECTFUL actions (panic/relay) additionally runs the envelope's\n18\t    strict monotonic-nonce replay gate (`consume(effectful=True)`). The owner trust-root is NEVER used\n19\t    to sign anything here — the phone signs, the server only verifies.\n20\t\n21\t  • THE ALLOWLIST. The anti-rebind Host/Origin allowlist is derived from the REAL bound address (plus\n22\t    the loopback pair when bound to loopback, for dev), not a hardcoded 127.0.0.1.\n23\t\n24\tEverything sensitive stays off the minimal frames: `/api/pending` and the SSE stream carry only\n25\t`{seq, tier, kind}` — never a subject, never a payload, never a secret. `serve()` now wraps the\n26\ttransport in an owner-PINNED self-signed TLS cert (the phone PWA needs a secure context — https or\n27\tlocalhost — to install and register a service worker; over a bare WireGuard IP that means self-signed\n28\tTLS whose sha256 fingerprint the owner pins once, stable across restarts). `build_server` itself\n29\tstays plain HTTP so the in-tunnel test harness is unchanged and TLS can be terminated upstream if\n30\tpreferred. Offense-free.\"\"\"\n31\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n32\t\n33\timport base64\n34\timport ipaddress\n35\timport json\n36\timport os\n37\timport time\n38\tfrom http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer\n39\tfrom pathlib import Path\n40\tfrom urllib.parse import parse_qs, urlparse\n41\t\n42\tfrom ..config import SPINE_PATH\n43\tfrom ..governor.identity import owner_pubkey\n44\tfrom ..mesh import authorized_devices\n45\tfrom ..spine.store import SpineStore\n46\tfrom ..spine.verify import verify_record\n47\tfrom .daemon import BridgeDaemon, bind_ok\n48\tfrom .envelope import consume, verify_envelope\n49\tfrom .notifier import PushNotifier\n50\t\n51\t_WEBAPP = Path(__file__).parent / \"webapp\"       # the PWA is a later slice — served if present, else 404\n52\t_CSP = \"default-src 'self'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'\"\n53\t_DEFAULT_PORT = 8722\n54\t_TS_WINDOW = 120.0                               # ± seconds a request timestamp may differ from the server clock\n55\t\n56\t# Each network endpoint declares the ONE envelope action that authorizes it. Binding the\n57\t# authenticated action to the endpoint stops a captured lesser-scoped envelope (e.g. `read:pending`,\n58\t# which leaks only {seq,tier,kind}) from being replayed against a more-sensitive endpoint (e.g.\n59\t# `read:recall`, which surfaces the owner's on-screen OCR history). `/api/graph` piggybacks on the\n60\t# `read:snapshot` scope: graph returns only aggregate health, strictly LESS sensitive than the\n61\t# snapshot, so a snapshot-scoped envelope reaching it is not an escalation (and a lesser envelope\n62\t# still cannot reach it).\n63\t_READ_ACTION = {\n64\t    \"/api/pending\": \"read:pending\",\n65\t    \"/api/snapshot\": \"read:snapshot\",\n66\t    \"/api/graph\": \"read:snapshot\",\n67\t    \"/api/stream\": \"read:stream\",\n68\t    \"/api/recall\": \"read:recall\",\n69\t}\n70\t\n71\t\n72\tclass BridgeServer(ThreadingHTTPServer):\n73\t    daemon_threads = True\n74\t\n75\t    def __init__(self, addr, handler, *, spine_path, trusted_pubkey=None, clock=None,\n76\t                 ts_window: float = _TS_WINDOW):\n77\t        host = addr[0]\n78\t        if not bind_ok(host):\n79\t            raise ValueError(\n80\t                f\"refusing to bind {host!r}: the bridge binds loopback or a PRIVATE (WireGuard) \"\n81\t                f\"address only — never 0.0.0.0 / an unspecified / a public address\")\n82\t        super().__init__(addr, handler)\n83\t        self.spine_path = Path(spine_path)\n84\t        # the owner PUBLIC key is the trust anchor (injectable for tests); NO private key ever lives here\n85\t        self.trusted_pubkey = trusted_pubkey if trusted_pubkey is not None else owner_pubkey()\n86\t        self.clock = clock or time.time          # injectable wallclock so the freshness gate is deterministic\n87\t        self.ts_window = ts_window\n88\t        port = self.server_address[1]            # the ACTUAL bound port (correct even for port 0)\n89\t        hosts = {f\"{host}:{port}\"}\n90\t        origins = {f\"http://{host}:{port}\"}\n91\t        if ipaddress.ip_address(host).is_loopback:   # dev convenience only — not added for a WG bind\n92\t            hosts |= {f\"127.0.0.1:{port}\", f\"localhost:{port}\"}\n93\t            origins |= {f\"http://127.0.0.1:{port}\", f\"http://localhost:{port}\"}\n94\t        self.allowed_hosts = frozenset(hosts)\n95\t        self.allowed_origins = frozenset(origins)\n96\t\n97\t    def store(self) -> SpineStore:\n98\t        return SpineStore(self.spine_path)       # fresh read each request (cheap, current)\n99\t\n100\t    def daemon(self) -> BridgeDaemon:\n101\t        return BridgeDaemon(self.store(), trusted_pubkey=self.trusted_pubkey)\n102\t\n103\t\n104\tdef _json_bytes(obj) -> bytes:\n105\t    return json.dumps(obj, ensure_ascii=False).encode(\"utf-8\")\n106\t\n107\t\n108\tclass Handler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):\n109\t    server_version = \"sigil-bridge/1.0\"\n110\t    timeout = 30                                  # per-connection socket timeout (no hung reader)\n111\t    _MAX_BODY = 65536                             # bodies are tiny; cap to avoid a Content-Length hang/alloc\n112\t\n113\t    # never log auth (an envelope can ride in ?env= for SSE; never write it to a log)\n114\t    def log_message(self, fmt, *args):  # noqa: A003\n115\t        pass\n116\t\n117\t    # --- request helpers --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n118\t    def _query(self) -> dict:\n119\t        return parse_qs(urlparse(self.path).query)\n120\t\n121\t    def _authorized_now(self):\n122\t        \"\"\"The owner-minted authorized-device set, recomputed PER REQUEST — a revoke takes effect now.\"\"\"\n123\t        return authorized_devices(self.server.store(), self.server.trusted_pubkey)\n124\t\n125\t    def _envelope_payload(self):\n126\t        \"\"\"The device envelope: base64url of canonical JSON, in `X-SIGIL-Envelope` (GET/POST) or the\n127\t        `?env=` query (SSE, which cannot set a header). Returns the payload dict, or None if absent/\n128\t        malformed (fail-closed — the caller denies).\"\"\"\n129\t        raw = self.headers.get(\"X-SIGIL-Envelope\") or (self._query().get(\"env\", [\"\"])[0])\n130\t        if not raw:\n131\t            return None\n132\t        try:\n133\t            data = base64.urlsafe_b64decode(raw + \"=\" * (-len(raw) % 4))\n134\t            payload = json.loads(data)\n135\t        except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 — any decode/parse failure is an unauthenticated request\n136\t            return None\n137\t        return payload if isinstance(payload, dict) else None\n138\t\n139\t    def _fresh(self, core) -> bool:\n140\t        \"\"\"The server-layer wallclock freshness window (the envelope module is pure — it never reads a\n141\t        clock — so freshness lives HERE). A non-numeric / far-off timestamp fails closed.\"\"\"\n142\t        try:\n143\t            ts = float(core.get(\"ts\"))\n144\t        except (TypeError, ValueError):\n145\t            return False\n146\t        return abs(self.server.clock() - ts) <= self.server.ts_window\n147\t\n148\t    def _authed(self, action: str):\n149\t        \"\"\"Authenticate a READ: verify the device signature, apply freshness, bind the action to this\n150\t        endpoint. Reads are side-effect-free, so they are NOT receipted (no spine write per GET) — the\n151\t        freshness window is their replay bound. Returns the core, or None after sending a deny.\"\"\"\n152\t        payload = self._envelope_payload()\n153\t        if payload is None:\n154\t            self._deny(401, \"missing/invalid device envelope\")\n155\t            return None\n156\t        ok, core = verify_envelope(payload, self._authorized_now())\n157\t        if not ok:\n158\t            self._deny(401, f\"unauthenticated: {core}\")\n159\t            return None\n160\t        if not self._fresh(core):\n161\t            self._deny(401, \"stale request (timestamp outside freshness window)\")\n162\t            return None\n163\t        if core.get(\"action\") != action:\n164\t            self._deny(403, \"envelope action does not authorize this endpoint\")\n165\t            return None\n166\t        return core\n167\t\n168\t    def _authed_effectful(self, action: str):\n169\t        \"\"\"Authenticate an EFFECTFUL request (panic/relay): verify + freshness + endpoint-bind, then run\n170\t        the envelope's strict monotonic-nonce replay gate and receipt it on the spine\n171\t        (`consume(effectful=True)`). A replayed/stale-nonce envelope is refused. Returns core or None.\"\"\"\n172\t        payload = self._envelope_payload()\n173\t        if payload is None:\n174\t            self._deny(401, \"missing/invalid device envelope\")\n175\t            return None\n176\t        authorized = self._authorized_now()\n177\t        ok, core = verify_envelope(payload, authorized)\n178\t        if not ok:\n179\t            self._deny(401, f\"unauthenticated: {core}\")\n180\t            return None\n181\t        if not self._fresh(core):\n182\t            self._deny(401, \"stale request (timestamp outside freshness window)\")\n183\t            return None\n184\t        if core.get(\"action\") != action:\n185\t            self._deny(403, \"envelope action does not authorize this endpoint\")\n186\t            return None\n187\t        try:\n188\t            return consume(self.server.store(), payload, authorized, effectful=True)\n189\t        except ValueError as e:                   # replay: nonce not fresh (fail-closed)\n190\t            self._deny(409, f\"refused: {str(e)[:120]}\")\n191\t            return None\n192\t\n193\t    def _rebind_ok(self) -> bool:\n194\t        \"\"\"The anti-DNS-rebinding gate (ui `_action_ok` logic, minus the token — the envelope is the\n195\t        credential): the `Host` must be in the WG-derived allowlist, an `Origin`, if present, must\n196\t        EXACT-match an allowed origin (a prefix like `http://IP:PORT.evil.com` must NOT pass), and a\n197\t        `Referer`, if present, must sit under an allowed origin. Applied to the whole POST action\n198\t        plane as defense-in-depth.\"\"\"\n199\t        if self.headers.get(\"Host\", \"\") not in self.server.allowed_hosts:\n200\t            return False\n201\t        o = self.headers.get(\"Origin\") or \"\"\n202\t        ref = self.headers.get(\"Referer\") or \"\"\n203\t        if o and o not in self.server.allowed_origins:\n204\t            return False\n205\t        if ref and not any(ref.startswith(a + \"/\") or ref == a for a in self.server.allowed_origins):\n206\t            return False\n207\t        return True\n208\t\n209\t    # --- response helpers -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n210\t    def _send(self, code: int, body: bytes, ctype=\"application/json\"):\n211\t        self.send_response(code)\n212\t        self.send_header(\"Content-Type\", ctype)\n213\t        self.send_header(\"Content-Length\", str(len(body)))\n214\t        self.send_header(\"Content-Security-Policy\", _CSP)\n215\t        self.send_header(\"X-Content-Type-Options\", \"nosniff\")\n216\t        self.send_header(\"Referrer-Policy\", \"no-referrer\")\n217\t        self.end_headers()\n218\t        try:\n219\t            self.wfile.write(body)\n220\t        except (BrokenPipeError, ConnectionResetError):\n221\t            pass\n222\t\n223\t    def _json(self, obj, code=200):\n224\t        self._send(code, _json_bytes(obj))\n225\t\n226\t    def _deny(self, code=403, msg=\"forbidden\"):\n227\t        self._json({\"error\": msg}, code)\n228\t\n229\t    # --- GET (read plane) -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n230\t    def do_GET(self):\n231\t        path = urlparse(self.path).path\n232\t        if path in (\"/\", \"/index.html\"):\n233\t            return self._serve_index()\n234\t        if path.startswith(\"/static/\"):\n235\t            return self._serve_static(path[len(\"/static/\"):])\n236\t        if not path.startswith(\"/api/\"):\n237\t            return self._deny(404, \"not found\")\n238\t        if path == \"/api/stream\":\n239\t            return self._sse()                    # SSE authenticates via ?env= inside _authed\n240\t        if path == \"/api/pending\":\n241\t            if self._authed(_READ_ACTION[path]) is None:\n242\t                return\n243\t            return self._json({\"pending\": self.server.daemon().pending()})   # {seq,tier,kind} only\n244\t        if path == \"/api/snapshot\":\n245\t            if self._authed(_READ_ACTION[path]) is None:\n246\t                return\n247\t            from ..dashboard import snapshot\n248\t            return self._json(snapshot(self.server.store()))\n249\t        if path == \"/api/graph\":\n250\t            if self._authed(_READ_ACTION[path]) is None:\n251\t                return\n252\t            return self._graph()\n253\t        if path.startswith(\"/api/record/\"):\n254\t            if self._authed(\"read:record\") is None:\n255\t                return\n256\t            return self._record(path.rsplit(\"/\", 1)[-1])\n257\t        if path == \"/api/recall\":\n258\t            if self._authed(_READ_ACTION[path]) is None:\n259\t                return\n260\t            subject = self._query().get(\"subject\", [\"\"])[0]\n261\t            return self._json({\"subject\": subject, \"recall\": self.server.daemon().recall(subject)})\n262\t        return self._deny(404, \"unknown endpoint\")\n263\t\n264\t    def _serve_index(self):\n265\t        idx = _WEBAPP / \"index.html\"\n266\t        if not idx.is_file():\n267\t            return self._deny(404, \"bridge PWA not built yet\")   # graceful — the webapp is a later slice\n268\t        try:\n269\t            self._send(200, idx.read_bytes(), ctype=\"text/html; charset=utf-8\")\n270\t        except OSError:\n271\t            self._deny(404, \"not found\")\n272\t\n273\t    def _serve_static(self, sub: str):\n274\t        base = _WEBAPP.resolve()\n275\t        target = (base / sub).resolve()\n276\t        if base != target and base not in target.parents:        # traversal guard (allowlist by containment)\n277\t            return self._deny(404, \"not found\")\n278\t        if not target.is_file():\n279\t            return self._deny(404, \"not found\")                  # includes: webapp dir absent (later slice)\n280\t        name = target.name\n281\t        ctype = (\"application/javascript\" if name.endswith(\".js\")\n282\t                 else \"text/css\" if name.endswith(\".css\")\n283\t                 else \"text/html\" if name.endswith(\".html\")\n284\t                 else \"application/octet-stream\")\n285\t        try:\n286\t            self._send(200, target.read_bytes(), ctype=f\"{ctype}; charset=utf-8\")\n287\t        except OSError:\n288\t            self._deny(404, \"not found\")\n289\t\n290\t    def _record(self, raw):\n291\t        try:\n292\t            seq = int(raw)\n293\t        except ValueError:\n294\t            return self._deny(400, \"bad seq\")\n295\t        rec = self.server.store().get(seq)\n296\t        if rec is None:\n297\t            return self._json({\"error\": \"no such record\", \"seq\": seq,\n298\t                               \"note\": \"no grounded record — not fabricated\"}, 404)\n299\t        ok, reason = verify_record(rec)                          # re-verify the atom LIVE (prove-don't-guess)\n300\t        self._json({\"seq\": rec.seq, \"kind\": rec.kind, \"source\": rec.source, \"actor\": rec.actor,\n301\t                    \"ts\": rec.ts, \"entry_hash\": rec.entry_hash, \"prev_hash\": rec.prev_hash,\n302\t                    \"payload\": rec.payload, \"integrity_ok\": ok, \"integrity_reason\": reason})\n303\t\n304\t    def _graph(self):\n305\t        try:\n306\t            from ..graph import health\n307\t            self._json({\"health\": health()})\n308\t        except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001 — graph may not be built yet (ImportError included)\n309\t            self._json({\"error\": \"graph unavailable\", \"note\": str(e)[:200]})\n310\t\n311\t    def _sse(self):\n312\t        if self._authed(\"read:stream\") is None:                  # deny already sent (401/403)\n313\t            return\n314\t        self.send_response(200)\n315\t        self.send_header(\"Content-Type\", \"text/event-stream\")\n316\t        self.send_header(\"Cache-Control\", \"no-cache\")\n317\t        self.send_header(\"Content-Security-Policy\", _CSP)\n318\t        self.send_header(\"X-Content-Type-Options\", \"nosniff\")\n319\t        self.send_header(\"Referrer-Policy\", \"no-referrer\")\n320\t        self.end_headers()\n321\t        try:\n322\t            since = int(self._query().get(\"since\", [\"-1\"])[0])\n323\t        except (ValueError, TypeError):\n324\t            since = -1                                            # any malformed cursor → from genesis\n325\t        notifier = PushNotifier(self.server.store(), since_seq=since)\n326\t        try:\n327\t            while True:\n328\t                sent = False\n329\t                for ev in notifier.poll():\n330\t                    frame = {\"seq\": ev[\"seq\"], \"tier\": ev[\"tier\"], \"kind\": ev[\"kind\"]}   # minimal — no subject\n331\t                    self.wfile.write(f\"data: {json.dumps(frame, ensure_ascii=False)}\\n\\n\".encode(\"utf-8\"))\n332\t                    sent = True\n333\t                self.wfile.write(b\": hb\\n\\n\")                     # heartbeat / flush\n334\t                self.wfile.flush()\n335\t                if not sent:\n336\t                    time.sleep(0.25)\n337\t        except (BrokenPipeError, ConnectionResetError, OSError):\n338\t            return                                                # client closed — end the stream\n339\t\n340\t    # --- POST (action plane) ----------------------------------------------------------------------\n341\t    def do_POST(self):\n342\t        path = urlparse(self.path).path\n343\t        if path not in (\"/api/action\", \"/api/panic\", \"/api/relay\", \"/api/gesture/arm\"):\n344\t            return self._deny(404, \"not found\")\n345\t        length = int(self.headers.get(\"Content-Length\", \"0\") or \"0\")\n346\t        if length > self._MAX_BODY:                              # cap the body (no CL hang / alloc)\n347\t            return self._deny(413, \"body too large\")\n348\t        body_bytes = self.rfile.read(length) if length else b\"\"\n349\t        if not self._rebind_ok():                               # anti-rebind gate on the whole action plane\n350\t            return self._deny(403, \"action denied (origin / host — possible DNS rebinding)\")\n351\t        if path == \"/api/action\":\n352\t            return self._device_action(body_bytes)\n353\t        if path == \"/api/gesture/arm\":\n354\t            return self._device_arm(body_bytes)\n355\t        # panic / relay — effectful, envelope-authenticated with the strict nonce replay gate\n356\t        action = \"panic\" if path == \"/api/panic\" else \"relay\"\n357\t        core = self._authed_effectful(action)\n358\t        if core is None:\n359\t            return\n360\t        if action == \"panic\":\n361\t            return self._json({\"ok\": True, \"seq\": self.server.daemon().panic_engage(by=\"phone\")})\n362\t        text = str((core.get(\"args\") or {}).get(\"text\", \"\"))     # the relayed command is INSIDE the signed core\n363\t        return self._json({\"ok\": True, \"reply\": self.server.daemon().relay(text)})\n364\t\n365\t    def _device_action(self, body_bytes):\n366\t        \"\"\"The phone posts its OWN device-signed `governor.approval` payload. The SERVER only VERIFIES\n367\t        (it never signs) — `submit_device_approval` accepts it iff the signing device is currently\n368\t        authorized AND the signature verifies; the signed `target_seq` binds, so no nonce is needed.\"\"\"\n369\t        try:\n370\t            body = json.loads(body_bytes or b\"{}\")\n371\t        except (ValueError, TypeError) as e:\n372\t            return self._deny(400, f\"bad request: {e}\")\n373\t        if not isinstance(body, dict):\n374\t            return self._deny(400, \"bad request: body must be a JSON object\")\n375\t        try:\n376\t            seq = self.server.daemon().submit_device_approval(body)\n377\t        except ValueError as e:                                  # unauthorized / forged / not-an-approval\n378\t            return self._deny(403, f\"approval refused: {str(e)[:200]}\")\n379\t        except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001 — never leak internals as a 500\n380\t            return self._deny(400, f\"action failed: {str(e)[:200]}\")\n381\t        self._json({\"ok\": True, \"seq\": seq})\n382\t\n383\t    def _device_arm(self, body_bytes):\n384\t        \"\"\"The phone posts its OWN device-signed gesture arm request. The SERVER only VERIFIES + RECORDS\n385\t        it (`submit_arm_request`, fail-closed on unauthorized/forged); the live SessionGate re-verifies\n386\t        and enforces freshness / kill-switch / single-session / TTL when it consumes it. Recording is\n387\t        necessary-but-not-sufficient to arm — the trust boundary is `arm_by_device`, not this endpoint.\"\"\"\n388\t        try:\n389\t            body = json.loads(body_bytes or b\"{}\")\n390\t        except (ValueError, TypeError) as e:\n391\t            return self._deny(400, f\"bad request: {e}\")\n392\t        if not isinstance(body, dict):\n393\t            return self._deny(400, \"bad request: body must be a JSON object\")\n394\t        try:\n395\t            seq = self.server.daemon().submit_arm_request(body)\n396\t        except ValueError as e:                                  # unauthorized / forged / not-an-arm-request\n397\t            return self._deny(403, f\"arm refused: {str(e)[:200]}\")\n398\t        except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001 — never leak internals as a 500\n399\t            return self._deny(400, f\"arm failed: {str(e)[:200]}\")\n400\t        self._json({\"ok\": True, \"seq\": seq})\n401\t\n402\t\n403\tdef build_server(*, addr: str, port: int = _DEFAULT_PORT, spine_path=None, trusted_pubkey=None,\n404\t                 clock=None) -> BridgeServer:\n405\t    \"\"\"Build (but do not run) the bridge server bound to `addr` (asserted `bind_ok`). `trusted_pubkey`\n406\t    and `clock` are injectable for deterministic tests; both default to the real owner identity / wallclock.\"\"\"\n407\t    return BridgeServer((addr, port), Handler,\n408\t                        spine_path=Path(spine_path) if spine_path else SPINE_PATH,\n409\t                        trusted_pubkey=trusted_pubkey, clock=clock)\n410\t\n411\t\n412\tdef _write_secure(path: Path, data: bytes) -> None:\n413\t    \"\"\"Write `data` to `path` created 0600 up-front (no world-readable window) — the vault/secrets\n414\t    secure-file idiom (`os.open(..., 0o600)`).\"\"\"\n415\t    fd = os.open(str(path), os.O_WRONLY | os.O_CREAT | os.O_TRUNC, 0o600)\n416\t    with os.fdopen(fd, \"wb\") as fh:\n417\t        fh.write(data)\n418\t\n419\t\n420\tdef _fingerprint(cert) -> str:\n421\t    \"\"\"The pinned identity: sha256 of the DER cert, hex, colon-grouped byte pairs (openssl-style,\n422\t    upper-case) — what the owner eyeball-matches on the phone once.\"\"\"\n423\t    from cryptography.hazmat.primitives import hashes\n424\t    h = cert.fingerprint(hashes.SHA256()).hex().upper()      # fingerprint() == sha256 of the DER encoding\n425\t    return \":\".join(h[i:i + 2] for i in range(0, len(h), 2))\n426\t\n427\t\n428\tdef ensure_bridge_cert(addr: str) -> tuple[str, str, str]:\n429\t    \"\"\"Mint (or REUSE) a minimal owner-pinned self-signed TLS cert for the bridge bound to `addr`,\n430\t    returning `(certfile, keyfile, sha256_fingerprint)`.\n431\t\n432\t    The cert carries `addr` as its CN and (when `addr` is an IP — always true for a `bind_ok` bridge)\n433\t    as an `x509.IPAddress` SubjectAlternativeName, so an IP-literal TLS connection matches. Cert+key\n434\t    live under `SIGIL_HOME/bridge/` (dir 0700, files 0600). If a cert already exists FOR THIS ADDR it\n435\t    is REUSED — the fingerprint is therefore STABLE across restarts, so the owner pins it exactly once\n436\t    (a changed fingerprint later means a MITM / a wiped key, not a benign restart). An EC P-256 key is\n437\t    used (easy for `cryptography`, accepted by `ssl`). Validity ~2 years; a real wallclock is fine here\n438\t    (this is TLS notBefore/notAfter, not spine or learning math).\"\"\"\n439\t    import datetime\n440\t    import ipaddress as _ip\n441\t\n442\t    from cryptography import x509\n443\t    from cryptography.hazmat.primitives import hashes, serialization\n444\t    from cryptography.hazmat.primitives.asymmetric import ec\n445\t    from cryptography.x509.oid import NameOID\n446\t\n447\t    from ..config import SIGIL_HOME                          # read at call-time (patchable in tests)\n448\t\n449\t    bridge_dir = Path(SIGIL_HOME) / \"bridge\"\n450\t    bridge_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n451\t    try:\n452\t        os.chmod(bridge_dir, 0o700)                          # owner-only, like the spine/keys dirs\n453\t    except OSError:\n454\t        pass\n455\t    tag = addr.replace(\":\", \"_\").replace(\"/\", \"_\").replace(\"%\", \"_\")   # one stable cert per bind addr\n456\t    certfile = bridge_dir / f\"cert-{tag}.pem\"\n457\t    keyfile = bridge_dir / f\"key-{tag}.pem\"\n458\t\n459\t    if certfile.is_file() and keyfile.is_file():             # REUSE → stable fingerprint (pin once)\n460\t        cert = x509.load_pem_x509_certificate(certfile.read_bytes())\n461\t        return str(certfile), str(keyfile), _fingerprint(cert)\n462\t\n463\t    key = ec.generate_private_key(ec.SECP256R1())\n464\t    name = x509.Name([x509.NameAttribute(NameOID.COMMON_NAME, addr)])\n465\t    try:                                                     # bind addr is an IP by construction (bind_ok)\n466\t        san = x509.SubjectAlternativeName([x509.IPAddress(_ip.ip_address(addr))])\n467\t    except ValueError:                                       # defensive: a hostname would use a DNS SAN\n468\t        san = x509.SubjectAlternativeName([x509.DNSName(addr)])\n469\t    now = datetime.datetime.now(datetime.timezone.utc)       # real wallclock (tz-aware; TLS validity, not spine math)\n470\t    cert = (\n471\t        x509.CertificateBuilder()\n472\t        .subject_name(name)\n473\t        .issuer_name(name)                                   # self-signed: issuer == subject\n474\t        .public_key(key.public_key())\n475\t        .serial_number(x509.random_serial_number())\n476\t        .not_valid_before(now - datetime.timedelta(minutes=5))   # small skew tolerance\n477\t        .not_valid_after(now + datetime.timedelta(days=730))     # ~2 years\n478\t        .add_extension(san, critical=False)\n479\t        .add_extension(x509.BasicConstraints(ca=False, path_length=None), critical=True)\n480\t        .sign(key, hashes.SHA256())\n481\t    )\n482\t    _write_secure(certfile, cert.public_bytes(serialization.Encoding.PEM))\n483\t    _write_secure(keyfile, key.private_bytes(serialization.Encoding.PEM,\n484\t                                             serialization.PrivateFormat.PKCS8,\n485\t                                             serialization.NoEncryption()))\n486\t    return str(certfile), str(keyfile), _fingerprint(cert)\n487\t\n488\t\n489\tdef serve(*, addr: str, port: int = _DEFAULT_PORT, spine_path=None, tls: bool = True) -> None:\n490\t    \"\"\"Run the WireGuard-bound bridge transport. With `tls=True` (default) it wraps the listening\n491\t    socket in an owner-pinned self-signed TLS cert (`ensure_bridge_cert`) BEFORE serving, printing the\n492\t    `https://` bind URL + the sha256 fingerprint to pin on the phone. With `tls=False` it serves plain\n493\t    HTTP (degraded: no secure context → the PWA cannot install / register a service worker) and prints\n494\t    an honest warning. `build_server` stays plain — TLS lives only here.\"\"\"\n495\t    srv = build_server(addr=addr, port=port, spine_path=spine_path)\n496\t    bound = srv.server_address[1]\n497\t    if tls:\n498\t        import ssl\n499\t        certfile, keyfile, fp = ensure_bridge_cert(addr)\n500\t        ctx = ssl.SSLContext(ssl.PROTOCOL_TLS_SERVER)\n501\t        ctx.load_cert_chain(certfile, keyfile)\n502\t        srv.socket = ctx.wrap_socket(srv.socket, server_side=True)   # wrap the listener before serve_forever\n503\t        print(f\"  SIGIL bridge → https://{addr}:{bound}/   (WireGuard-bound; loopback/private only — NO wire secret)\")\n504\t        print(f\"  TLS fingerprint (sha256): {fp}\")\n505\t        print(\"    ↳ PIN this once on the phone — it is stable across restarts; a later change means MITM\")\n506\t    else:\n507\t        print(f\"  SIGIL bridge → http://{addr}:{bound}/   (WireGuard-bound; loopback/private only — NO wire secret)\")\n508\t        print(\"  WARNING: --no-tls → plain HTTP, NO secure context: the phone CANNOT install the PWA or\")\n509\t        print(\"           register a service worker. Use TLS unless you terminate it upstream in the tunnel.\")\n510\t    print(\"  pair a phone (owner, at the desktop):  sigil mesh authorize <device-id> <device-pubkey>\")\n511\t    print(\"  every request must carry an authorized-device signature (X-SIGIL-Envelope) — there is no token\")\n512\t    try:\n513\t        srv.serve_forever()\n514\t    except KeyboardInterrupt:\n515\t        srv.shutdown()\n516\t"}]},"uuid":"aa3aa054-ea5c-49c3-8d90-0c05c34c8ae8","timestamp":"2026-07-19T00:30:57.344Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"1d586ae7-e2ef-482c-b632-1414b4accfef","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"ab88083f-5986-40c1-a3d6-9c0309cd87cd","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"ab85e5582d27cf290","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011CdAUPNSa2UhB2Yb1EETAu","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01WjSRWvgfimzGXhvAsXeJny","name":"Read","input":{"file_path":"/home/kali/sigil/sigil/bridge/daemon.py"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":14160,"cache_read_input_tokens":33082,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":14160,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdAUPLgtzkjWYePEABiNX","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","type":"assistant","uuid":"e9cf4d84-2579-4fb9-953d-9ed361efc7c1","timestamp":"2026-07-19T00:31:01.738Z","effort":"xhigh","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
{"parentUuid":"e9cf4d84-2579-4fb9-953d-9ed361efc7c1","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"c7c0b4cd-e6c6-4488-a68b-ca4f4169315f","agentId":"ab85e5582d27cf290","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01WjSRWvgfimzGXhvAsXeJny","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"BridgeDaemon (Phase 7, WS-D D-v) — the desktop-side API the phone talks to over WireGuard. It\n2\treuses the owner-signed cores: the queue (`approvals.pending` with the authorized-device set), a\n3\tDEVICE-signed approval (accepted only while that device is authorized), a PANIC halt (any engage is\n4\tfail-safe), and a KERNEL command relay (`KernelDispatch` → the same WARDEN gate as voice/UI).\n5\t\n6\tThis module is the bridge LIBRARY CORE + API (the doctrine + crypto). The network TRANSPORT — an\n7\tHTTP server over WireGuard, reusing the WS-C two-plane server pattern bound to a `bind_ok` address —\n8\tis a documented NEXT slice, not shipped here. `bind_ok` is the bind predicate that transport MUST\n9\tuse: loopback or a PRIVATE (WireGuard) address only — NEVER 0.0.0.0 / a public address.\"\"\"\n10\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n11\t\n12\timport ipaddress\n13\tfrom typing import List, Optional\n14\t\n15\tfrom ..spine.store import SpineStore\n16\t\n17\t\n18\tdef bind_ok(addr: str) -> bool:\n19\t    \"\"\"True iff `addr` is safe to bind: loopback or a PRIVATE (e.g. WireGuard) address — never\n20\t    0.0.0.0 / a public / an unspecified address.\"\"\"\n21\t    try:\n22\t        ip = ipaddress.ip_address(addr)\n23\t    except ValueError:\n24\t        return False\n25\t    if ip.is_unspecified:                          # 0.0.0.0 / :: → refuse\n26\t        return False\n27\t    return ip.is_loopback or ip.is_private\n28\t\n29\t\n30\tclass BridgeDaemon:\n31\t    def __init__(self, store: Optional[SpineStore] = None, *, trusted_pubkey: Optional[str] = None):\n32\t        from ..governor.identity import owner_pubkey\n33\t        self.store = store or SpineStore()\n34\t        # the owner trust anchor — the persisted identity by default (injectable for tests)\n35\t        self.trusted_pubkey = trusted_pubkey if trusted_pubkey is not None else owner_pubkey()\n36\t\n37\t    def _authorized(self):\n38\t        from ..mesh import authorized_devices\n39\t        return authorized_devices(self.store, self.trusted_pubkey)\n40\t\n41\t    def pending(self) -> List[dict]:\n42\t        \"\"\"The phone-facing queue — minimal fields only (no subject leaks over the tunnel).\"\"\"\n43\t        from ..agents.approvals import pending\n44\t        items = pending(self.store, self.trusted_pubkey, extra_pubkeys=self._authorized())\n45\t        return [{\"seq\": r.seq, \"tier\": r.payload.get(\"tier\"), \"kind\": r.kind} for r in items]\n46\t\n47\t    def recall(self, subject: str) -> Optional[dict]:\n48\t        \"\"\"Read-only remote RECALL — \"where did I last see X?\" — answered from the owner's own\n49\t        GROUNDED on-screen OCR history. Returns the perception-recall provenance dict VERBATIM\n50\t        (seq/entry_hash/when/frame_sha256/quote) or None: A0, read-only, no VLM and no paraphrase —\n51\t        the served `quote` is the owner's verbatim captured OCR line, never an advisory VLM lead.\n52\t\n53\t        This surfaces the owner's own on-screen text over the tunnel, so it leaks MORE than\n54\t        `pending()`'s minimal {seq,tier,kind} fields. The network SERVER (a later slice) MUST\n55\t        therefore gate this behind an authorized-device (device-signed) read request; this daemon\n56\t        method is only the read-only core.\"\"\"\n57\t        from ..perception.recall import recall\n58\t        return recall(self.store, subject)\n59\t\n60\t    def submit_device_approval(self, payload: dict) -> int:\n61\t        \"\"\"Append a DEVICE-signed approval the phone produced — ONLY if the signing device is\n62\t        currently authorized AND the signature verifies (fail-closed). A rogue/revoked device or a\n63\t        forged signature is refused; the signed target_seq binds (no replay).\"\"\"\n64\t        from ..agents.approvals import SIGNAL as APPROVAL_SIGNAL\n65\t        from ..agents.approvals import verify_approval\n66\t\n67\t        class _Rec:\n68\t            def __init__(self, p): self.payload = p\n69\t        authorized = self._authorized()\n70\t        if payload.get(\"signal\") != APPROVAL_SIGNAL:\n71\t            raise ValueError(\"not an approval record\")\n72\t        if payload.get(\"pubkey\") not in authorized:\n73\t            raise ValueError(\"signing device is not authorized\")\n74\t        if not verify_approval(_Rec(payload), self.trusted_pubkey, extra_pubkeys=authorized):\n75\t            raise ValueError(\"approval signature invalid\")\n76\t        return self.store.append(kind=\"event\", source=\"mesh\", actor=\"DEVICE\",\n77\t                                 payload={**payload, \"tier\": \"A0\", \"decision\": \"auto\"},\n78\t                                 supersedes_id=payload.get(\"target_seq\"))\n79\t\n80\t    def submit_arm_request(self, request: dict) -> int:\n81\t        \"\"\"Record a DEVICE-signed gesture arm request (the transport layer) — ONLY if the signing device\n82\t        is authorized AND the signature verifies (fail-closed, mirrors submit_device_approval). The live\n83\t        SessionGate RE-VERIFIES and enforces freshness / kill-switch / single-session / TTL when it\n84\t        consumes this via `arm_by_device`, so recording is necessary-but-not-sufficient to arm.\"\"\"\n85\t        from ..gesture.session import ARM_REQUEST, _ARM_CORE, arm_request_message\n86\t        from ..reuse import verify_one\n87\t        authorized = self._authorized()\n88\t        if request.get(\"signal\") != ARM_REQUEST:\n89\t            raise ValueError(\"not an arm request\")\n90\t        pub = request.get(\"pubkey\")\n91\t        if pub not in authorized:\n92\t            raise ValueError(\"signing device is not authorized\")\n93\t        core = {k: request.get(k) for k in _ARM_CORE}\n94\t        try:                                            # verify_one RAISES on a malformed-length sig — refuse cleanly\n95\t            ok = verify_one(pub, arm_request_message(core), request.get(\"sig\", \"\"))\n96\t        except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001\n97\t            ok = False\n98\t        if not ok:\n99\t            raise ValueError(\"arm request signature invalid\")\n100\t        return self.store.append(kind=\"event\", source=\"mesh\", actor=\"DEVICE\",\n101\t                                 payload={**request, \"tier\": \"A0\", \"decision\": \"auto\"})\n102\t\n103\t    def panic_engage(self, *, by: str = \"phone\") -> int:\n104\t        \"\"\"Halt the mesh from the phone. ANY engage halts (fail-safe) — no signature needed for the\n105\t        SAFE direction. Release stays owner-only at the desktop (the dangerous direction is signed).\"\"\"\n106\t        from ..governor.killswitch import SIGNAL as KS_SIGNAL\n107\t        return self.store.append(kind=\"event\", source=\"mesh\", actor=\"DEVICE\",\n108\t                                 payload={\"signal\": KS_SIGNAL, \"state\": \"engaged\", \"by\": by,\n109\t                                          \"reason\": \"panic halt from phone\", \"tier\": \"A0\", \"decision\": \"auto\"})\n110\t\n111\t    def relay(self, text: str) -> str:\n112\t        \"\"\"Relay a KERNEL command — the same T0-router + WARDEN gate + signed action log as voice/UI.\"\"\"\n113\t        from ..voice.dispatch import KernelDispatch\n114\t        return KernelDispatch().send(text)\n115\t"}]},"uuid":"d86cd2a9-3191-4de6-8f25-6d759bc92958","timestamp":"2026-07-19T00:31:01.752Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"e9cf4d84-2579-4fb9-953d-9ed361efc7c1","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
